Latest target-quality vector-comparison hardening closes the backend evidence-contract gap for Qdrant-enabled retrieval quality claims without schema or migration churn. `pnpm smoke:quality:target` now supports `--require-vector-comparison` / `TARGET_QUALITY_REQUIRE_VECTOR_COMPARISON=true` with pgvector baseline and external-vector candidate route expectations; collected evidence records sanitized route-mode counts and fails if the required route pair is not observed. `GET /api/v1/admin/target-quality/posture`, `client.admin.targetQualityPosture()`, OpenAPI, and the GA posture readback now expose only route-mode counts, comparison status/counts, and optional target flags, not replay queries, hit IDs, vector endpoints, collection names, vectors, evidence bodies, tokens, or secrets. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-target-quality-vector-comparison` / `GA_REQUIRE_TARGET_QUALITY_VECTOR_COMPARISON=true` rejects target-quality evidence missing pgvector-to-Qdrant comparison proof, and `pnpm ga:target-preflight` blocks early when that strict mode is requested with a single replay file or invalid route settings. Validation passed Prettier, script syntax checks, `pnpm smoke:quality:contract`, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract`, `pnpm smoke:ga:target-preflight-contract`, focused core/API-client checks and tests, OpenAPI route coverage, and strict checklist generation with `targetQualityVectorComparisonRequired=true`. Live representative-corpus execution in the selected deployment is still required before claiming target search-quality completion.

Latest Postgres operations GA hardening adds an optional strict Phase 34 gate for CloudNativePG or external hosted Postgres operational readiness without schema or migration churn. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-postgres-operations-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_POSTGRES_OPERATIONS_LIVE=true` now requires representative query-plan review, passed slow-query telemetry, passed lock telemetry, and accepted archival/partitioning decision evidence. `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps the gate to the three safe collection commands, requires `psql`, target tier/mode env vars, bounded telemetry thresholds, `POSTGRES_ARCHIVAL_DECISION`, and `DATABASE_URL`, and blocks external hosted Postgres unless the URL uses `sslmode=verify-full` while returning no URL, host, SQL, query text, lock statement, row, or secret values. Validation now covers synthetic positive/negative GA evidence and preflight contracts for non-representative query-plan evidence, missing representative telemetry calls, unaccepted archival decisions, redaction leaks, and weak hosted TLS. Product completion still requires running the collectors against the selected target database after representative traffic and mounting reviewed metadata-only evidence.

Latest Qdrant live GA hardening adds an optional strict Phase 32 release gate for deployments that claim target Qdrant external-vector isolation. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-qdrant-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_QDRANT_LIVE=true` now requires passed `romeo.qdrant-live-evidence.v1` from `dist/ci/qdrant-live-evidence.json`, and `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps that gate to `QDRANT_LIVE_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true QDRANT_API_KEY=$QDRANT_API_KEY pnpm smoke:qdrant:live -- --url $QDRANT_URL --collection $QDRANT_COLLECTION --namespace-policy $VECTOR_NAMESPACE_POLICY --partitioning-policy $VECTOR_PARTITIONING_POLICY --confirm-mutation --output dist/ci/qdrant-live-evidence.json`. The validator rejects dry-run evidence, `VECTOR_NAMESPACE_POLICY=none`, missing collection health, incomplete mutation/query/delete proof, missing namespace/partition/foreign-org trap exclusion, missing filter proof, and redaction leaks; preflight returns only safe URL origin, configured booleans, policy names, and redaction flags. Validation passed script syntax checks, Prettier, GA evidence-contract smoke, GA target-preflight contract smoke, GA target-plan contract smoke, docs command check, strict greenfield baseline review, default GA checklist generation, and `--require-qdrant-live` checklist/preflight generation. The default pgvector-only checklist remains 19 of 29 gates satisfied; the Qdrant-live-required checklist becomes 19 of 30 gates satisfied until target live evidence is collected. This has no schema or migration impact and keeps Qdrant release claims enforceable without adding migration files.

Latest RAG/vector readiness hardening closes a declaration-only high-isolation gap without schema or migration churn. `/api/v1/admin/readiness` now fails `VECTOR_ISOLATION_MODE=external_namespace_per_org`, `external_collection_per_org`, or `dedicated_vector_store_per_org` unless Qdrant routing is active and `QDRANT_LIVE_EVIDENCE_PATH` contains satisfied live `romeo.qdrant-live-evidence.v1` evidence whose namespace and partitioning policies match deployment config; `external_namespace_per_org` additionally requires `VECTOR_NAMESPACE_POLICY=org`. The readiness response returns only sanitized driver/configuration booleans, policy names, evidence status, and stable missing-prerequisite names, not Qdrant endpoints, collection names, secret refs, API keys, evidence paths, evidence bodies, namespace values, partition values, or vectors. Focused validation passed Prettier, `pnpm --filter @romeo/core test -- src/readiness.test.ts src/knowledge.test.ts`, `pnpm --filter @romeo/core check`, and OpenAPI route coverage.

Latest data-rights export hardening closes the local workspace-scoped background-job ledger gap without creating migration churn. `background_jobs` now has an optional first-class `workspace_id` in the locked greenfield baseline plus an org/workspace/created index; job-producing backend paths populate it for workflow browser automation, knowledge extraction/reindex/embedding jobs, run-context tool dispatch requests, tool approval/idempotency jobs, and delegated OAuth callback replay guards, while org-level webhook/notification retry and connector-level dispatch jobs remain org-scoped. Governed data exports now include all metadata-only job rows for org exports and only matching workspace-keyed job rows for workspace exports, with payloads, artifact keys, object-store keys, prompts, connector configs, operational logs, and backup locations still excluded. Validation passed focused Prettier, `pnpm --filter @romeo/core check`, `pnpm --filter @romeo/db check`, `pnpm --filter @romeo/api-client check`, `pnpm --filter @romeo/db db:generate` with no schema changes, focused core/DB/API-client tests, `pnpm validate:postgres -- --dry-run --output dist/ci/postgres-schema-workspace-job-export.json`, `pnpm check:openapi-route-coverage`, and `pnpm check:sdk-drift`. The migration inventory remains only `packages/db/migrations/0000_greenfield_baseline.sql`; no follow-on migration files were added.

Latest suspended-tenant evidence coverage hardening wires the workflow negative suite into `pnpm smoke:tenant-isolation-negative`, so the local Phase 33 evidence now proves the browser automation artifact-upload suspension path alongside direct model/eval/voice calls, runs, tools, connector syncs, scheduled connector/workflow creation, webhook/notification retry egress, knowledge jobs, browser workers, and uploads. The GA validator now requires `src/workflows.test.ts` in tenant-isolation evidence, and the synthetic GA evidence contract fixture matches that stricter command surface. Validation passed focused Prettier, script syntax checks, `pnpm smoke:tenant-isolation-negative -- --output dist/ci/tenant-isolation-negative-suite.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-suspended-tenant-expanded-boundary.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-suspended-tenant-expanded-boundary.json`, `pnpm docs:check-commands -- --output dist/ci/docs-command-check-suspended-tenant-expanded-boundary.json`, and `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output dist/ci/greenfield-baseline-suspended-tenant-expanded-boundary.json`. The draft checklist remains blocked by the same ten live target-environment gates, and no migration files were added.

Latest target analytics authorization backend work adds metadata-only representative target authz evidence readback without schema churn: `pnpm evidence:analytics-authz-live` writes reviewed `romeo.analytics-authz-live-evidence.v1` metadata, `ANALYTICS_AUTHZ_EVIDENCE_PATH` mounts it for Compose or Kubernetes, `GET /api/v1/admin/analytics/authz-posture` exposes sanitized posture, and `client.admin.analyticsAuthzPosture()` gives admin UI/SDK consumers a typed helper. The posture report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, subject counts, allow/deny counts, analytics readback counts, CSV hash counts, warning codes, and redaction booleans only. It intentionally does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, analytics CSV rows, eval inputs/outputs, human comments, usage metadata, job payloads, provider config, org/workspace names, user emails, API keys, tokens, secret refs, or secret values. Product completion still requires running the representative target admin/non-admin/cross-org/cross-workspace authorization checks in the selected deployment and mounting reviewed evidence.

Latest enterprise identity backend work makes browser OIDC login fully org-aware without adding storage or migrations. `GET /api/v1/auth/oidc/start` now accepts optional `orgId`, returns sanitized `orgId` and `providerId` metadata, and signs org/provider/issuer/nonce/code-verifier state so callback completion uses the same selected org-scoped provider settings rather than falling back to `org_default`. `client.startOidcLogin({ providerId, orgId, returnTo })` and `client.sessions.startOidcLogin(...)` expose the contract for the frontend auth-provider app-store page. Focused coverage proves an org-level Okta config is selected for PKCE start, callback validates through the org-specific client ID, the session user is provisioned into that org, OpenAPI documents the query parameter/readback, and no migration files were added.

Latest target identity evidence backend work adds metadata-only live identity evidence readback without schema churn: `pnpm evidence:identity-live` writes reviewed `romeo.identity-live-evidence.v1` metadata, `IDENTITY_LIVE_EVIDENCE_PATH` mounts it for Compose or Kubernetes, `GET /api/v1/admin/identity/live-posture` exposes sanitized posture, and `client.admin.identityLivePosture()` gives admin UI/SDK consumers a typed helper. The posture report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, provider/login counts, managed-secret backend counts, directory lookup/group-mapping/directory-sync counts, SCIM/deprovisioning lifecycle counts, access-review counts, warning codes, and redaction booleans only. It intentionally does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, provider endpoints, IdP responses, directory entries, LDAP DNs, SAML assertions, group names, email addresses, secret refs, token values, or secret values. Product completion still requires running the target Vault/secret-backend, IdP, customer-directory, group lifecycle, directory-sync, SCIM/deprovisioning, and access-review drills in the selected deployment and mounting reviewed evidence.

Latest identity and analytics live GA hardening adds optional strict Phase 23 and Phase 32 target-evidence gates without schema churn. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-identity-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_IDENTITY_LIVE=true` now requires passed live metadata-only identity evidence, and `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps it to `IDENTITY_LIVE_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true pnpm evidence:identity-live -- --output dist/ci/identity-live-evidence.json`. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-analytics-authz-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_ANALYTICS_AUTHZ_LIVE=true` now requires passed live metadata-only analytics authorization/export evidence, and preflight maps it to `ANALYTICS_AUTHZ_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true pnpm evidence:analytics-authz-live -- --output dist/ci/analytics-authz-live-evidence.json`. Validation passed script syntax checks, both evidence writers, GA evidence-contract smoke, GA target-preflight contract smoke, checklist/preflight/target-plan generation with both optional gates enabled, docs command check, strict greenfield baseline review, and focused Prettier. The optional gates report satisfied when reviewed evidence is present; the checklist remains blocked only by existing target-environment gates, product completion still requires executing the selected identity and analytics drills before recording reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest delegated OAuth redaction work removes raw provider account IDs and logins from public connection summaries and enterprise access-review delegated OAuth posture without adding storage or migrations. `GET /api/v1/delegated-oauth/connections`, `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/connections/{connectionId}/revoke`, TypeScript SDK connection contracts, OpenAPI schemas, and access-review reports now expose stable provider-account hashes plus provider-login presence/hash metadata only. Focused coverage asserts connection list/revoke and access-review JSON do not contain the raw GitHub account ID or login sentinels, while connector runtime storage can still use encrypted token envelopes and internal provider account identity for refresh/revoke binding.

Latest native chat branching backend work adds `POST /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/fork`, `client.chatApi.fork(...)`, and `client.forkChat(...)` without adding storage or migrations. The endpoint creates a same-workspace chat, copies source messages through an optional `throughMessageId` inclusive, optionally omits attachment metadata, and deliberately does not copy runs, comments, approvals, hidden tool/job payloads, or object-store keys. Focused coverage proves copied branch history and attachment metadata are readable through existing message APIs, later source messages are excluded, OpenAPI documents the route, and audit metadata contains only source IDs/counts rather than message text or attachment object-key sentinels.

Latest native assistant-message feedback backend work adds `GET /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/message-feedback`, `GET|POST /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/messages/{messageId}/feedback`, `client.messageFeedbackList(...)`, `client.messageFeedback(...)`, and `client.updateMessageFeedback(...)` without adding storage or migrations. The contract stores one caller feedback state per assistant message in the existing usage-event lifecycle with `chat.message.feedback`, supports efficient chat-level hydration, supports `positive`, `negative`, and `none` clear semantics, and returns only rating state, optional stable reason code, timestamps, and redaction flags. It intentionally rejects free-text feedback and user-message ratings; audit and usage metadata record message IDs, role, configured state, and reason-code presence without message content, reviewer identity, raw usage metadata, prompts, or response text.

Latest tenant lifecycle backend work adds global-admin organization administration without schema churn: `GET|POST /api/v1/admin/organizations`, `GET|PATCH /api/v1/admin/organizations/{orgId}`, suspend/reactivate, deletion-request, deletion-request cancel, deletion-finalization preview, deletion-finalization evidence, and deletion-finalization execute routes plus TypeScript SDK helpers. Provisioning creates an organization, default workspace, and optional initial local `org_admin` password credential; cross-org tenant admin is global-admin only; suspension and deletion requests reuse the existing abuse-control enforcement boundary so suspended tenants cannot create new file uploads or other cost-incurring work. That boundary now covers direct OpenAI-compatible chat/embedding requests, eval provider runs, voice provider requests, scheduled connector/workflow creation, browser automation artifact-upload registration, explicit webhook test/retry egress, and explicit notification retry egress in addition to runs, tools, dispatch, connector sync, workflow run/approval/resume, knowledge jobs, browser workers, and file uploads. Lifecycle, finalization-evidence, and purge-result state stores in `system_settings`; preview reports deletion request/suspension/evidence preconditions plus storage-class counts for Postgres records, tracked object-store artifacts, knowledge/vector rows, data-export packages, background jobs, audit logs, and external secret-store review. Evidence recording stores reviewed status plus optional evidence-reference hashes for Postgres purge planning, object-store purge planning, external-vector purge, backup retention, operational-log retention, support-bundle retention, and external secret-store review. Final execution requires exact org confirmation plus `confirmPermanentDeletion: true`, deletes app-tracked Postgres rows and tracked object-store artifacts after every control is satisfied, and records a sanitized purge result. Responses and audits contain reason codes/counts/booleans/control names/hashes only and explicitly omit raw evidence refs, evidence bodies, object keys, logs, secrets, and vector values. External vector stores, backups, operational logs, support bundles, and external secret stores remain governed deployment evidence controls.

Latest tenant purge evidence backend work adds metadata-only target purge evidence readback without schema churn: `pnpm evidence:tenant-purge` writes reviewed `romeo.tenant-purge-evidence.v1` metadata, `TENANT_PURGE_EVIDENCE_PATH` mounts it for Compose or Kubernetes, `GET /api/v1/admin/tenant-deletion/purge-evidence-posture` exposes sanitized posture, and `client.admin.tenantPurgeEvidencePosture()` gives the UI/admin SDK a typed helper. Target tenant purge can now be promoted to an optional strict Phase 33 GA gate with `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-tenant-purge-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_TENANT_PURGE_LIVE=true`; `pnpm ga:target-preflight` requires `TENANT_PURGE_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true` and reports sanitized review posture only. The posture report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, tenant purge counts, storage-class counts, retention-day metadata, warning codes, and redaction booleans only. It intentionally does not return mounted paths, evidence file bodies, object-store keys, vector values, backup locations, operational log bodies, support bundle bodies, or secret values. Product completion still requires running the target tenant purge and external vector/backup/log/support/secret-store reviews in the selected deployment and mounting the reviewed evidence.

Tenant lifecycle, tenant purge evidence, identity live evidence, and analytics authz live posture validation passed focused Prettier, evidence writer syntax/positive/negative checks, `@romeo/config`, `@romeo/core`, `@romeo/api-client`, and `@romeo/db` checks/tests, OpenAPI route coverage for 391 public API routes, Compose env contract smoke, Compose config render, Helm env-schema contract smoke, docs command-check, Kubernetes render smoke, no-op `@romeo/db db:generate`, OpenAPI export, Python SDK regeneration with 391 operations, SDK drift, strict greenfield baseline review, broad `pnpm check`, broad `pnpm lint`, broad `pnpm test`, and broad `pnpm build`. The migration inventory remains only `packages/db/migrations/0000_greenfield_baseline.sql`.

Latest native chat deletion backend work adds `GET /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/delete-preview`, `DELETE /api/v1/chats/{chatId}`, `client.deleteChatPreview(...)`, and `client.deleteChat(chatId, { confirmChatId })` without adding storage or migrations. The endpoints make deletion a first-party chat lifecycle operation while reusing the governed data-deletion repository path, exact confirmation, active legal-hold block, transactional delete-plus-audit boundary, and metadata-only count result. Focused coverage proves preview/delete counts, confirmation mismatch rejection, post-delete 404 readback, and sanitized `chat.delete` audit metadata without deleted titles or message bodies.

Latest native chat tag backend work adds `GET /api/v1/chat-tags`, `GET /api/v1/chat-tags/{tagSlug}/chats`, `GET|POST /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/tag-assignments`, `DELETE /api/v1/chats/{chatId}/tag-assignments/{tagSlug}`, and TypeScript helpers `client.chatTags(...)`, `client.taggedChats(...)`, `client.chatTagAssignments(...)`, `client.assignChatTag(...)`, and `client.removeChatTag(...)` without adding storage or migrations. The implementation reuses the existing user-scoped chat-tag repository tables behind Romeo-native non-OpenWebUI paths so compatibility mode can keep its same-path bridge routes. Focused coverage proves assignment, listing, filtering, orphan cleanup after removal, OpenAPI discovery, client paths, and audit metadata that stores tag IDs/slug hashes without tag names or chat titles.

Latest native collaboration channel backend work adds Romeo-owned `/api/v1/collaboration/channels` APIs for channel list/create/get/update/delete, direct-message creation, members, messages, threads, read state, pins, reactions, and SSE events, plus TypeScript helpers under `client.channels.*` and convenience methods such as `client.createChannel(...)`, `client.postChannelMessage(...)`, and `client.channelEvents(...)`. The implementation now uses Romeo-owned `collaboration_channels` and `collaboration_channel_members` storage plus `collaboration_channel_metadata` message parts in the greenfield baseline, while `/api/v1/channels/...` remains reserved for the optional reference bridge. Focused coverage proves private channel member isolation, member readback, message posting/read state/pin/reaction flows, OpenAPI discovery, client paths, and audit metadata that stores IDs/counts/flags and reaction-name hashes without channel names, descriptions, message bodies, or reaction labels. No new migration file was added.

Latest native workspace folder lifecycle work completes the Romeo-owned folder API with `GET|PATCH|DELETE /api/v1/folders/{folderId}`, richer `POST /api/v1/folders` support for parent/data/meta/expanded state, concrete OpenAPI `WorkspaceFolder` and `WorkspaceFolderItem` schemas, and TypeScript helpers `client.collaboration.folder(...)`, `updateFolder(...)`, and `deleteFolder(...)`. The implementation reuses the existing durable workspace-folder tables and resource-grant authorization, enforces per-workspace duplicate-name checks, rejects parent cycles, moves child folders to root on delete, removes only folder-item associations, and deliberately does not delete underlying chats, agents, or knowledge bases. Focused coverage proves create/get/update/move/delete behavior, duplicate and cycle rejection, OpenAPI discovery, client paths, and audit metadata that stores changed-field flags and counts without folder names, sidebar metadata, or chat titles. No schema or migration file was added.

Latest native agent detail work adds `GET /api/v1/agents/{agentId}`, OpenAPI coverage, `client.agent.get(agentId)`, and `client.agentById(agentId)` so the UI can hydrate an agent editor or selector directly instead of inferring detail state from workspace list responses. The route reuses the existing resource-grant-aware `agents:read` authorization boundary and returns the standard Romeo `{ data: Agent }` envelope. No schema or migration file was added.

Latest native knowledge-base metadata work adds `GET|PATCH /api/v1/knowledge-bases/{knowledgeBaseId}`, a named OpenAPI `KnowledgeBase` schema plus `UpdateKnowledgeBaseRequest`, repository-level `updateKnowledgeBase` coverage, and TypeScript helpers `client.knowledge.getBase(...)` and `client.knowledge.updateBase(...)`. The implementation reuses existing knowledge-base resource grants and workspace-active checks, supports description clearing with `description: null`, and audits only changed-field flags plus description presence without KB names or descriptions. No schema or migration file was added.

Latest browser automation live-evidence backend work adds `pnpm evidence:browser-automation-live` for reviewed `romeo.browser-automation-live-evidence.v1` files consumed by the existing `BROWSER_AUTOMATION_LIVE_EVIDENCE_PATH` posture readback. The generator records runner sandbox, target-origin-only context isolation, CNI/NetworkPolicy and DNS-rebinding denial, worker crash/retry, retention-worker execution, pod/worker log-redaction counts, and redaction flags only; it refuses `--status passed` when live mode, sandbox, network denial, crash/retry, retention, log-redaction, or redaction posture is inconsistent. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-browser-automation` / `GA_REQUIRE_BROWSER_AUTOMATION=true` now makes that live runner evidence a hard GA gate for deployments that enable browser automation, and `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps the optional gate to the evidence command plus a redacted `BROWSER_AUTOMATION_LIVE_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true` operator confirmation. The default GA checklist remains unchanged for deployments that keep browser automation disabled. The current posture contract exposes sanitized `mode` and `deployment` readback while still omitting runner URLs, evidence paths/bodies, raw task text, raw page content, artifact bytes, object-store keys, or secrets. Product completion still requires executing the selected live browser runner and mounting reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest network partition backend posture work adds metadata-only target network-partition evidence support without changing the database. Operators can generate reviewed `romeo.network-partition-evidence.v1` with `pnpm evidence:network-partition`, mount it through `NETWORK_PARTITION_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and read sanitized posture through `GET /api/v1/admin/network/partition-posture` plus `client.admin.networkPartitionPosture()`. The report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, partition counts, degraded/fail-closed API posture, worker backpressure and storm-prevention posture, recovery, alerting, CNI/NetworkPolicy context, warning codes, and redaction booleans only; it does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, network endpoints, pod IPs, packet captures, raw log lines, or secret values. Compose, Helm values/schema, OpenAPI, TypeScript SDK, and operator docs now expose the contract. Product completion still requires executing the selected target network-partition drill and mounting reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest secret rotation backend posture work adds metadata-only target secret-rotation drill evidence support without changing the database. Operators can generate reviewed `romeo.secret-rotation-drill-evidence.v1` with `pnpm evidence:secret-rotation-drill`, mount it through `SECRET_ROTATION_DRILL_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and read sanitized posture through `GET /api/v1/admin/secret-rotation/drill-posture` plus `client.admin.secretRotationDrillPosture()`. The report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, staged session-secret and webhook-signing cutover posture, local MFA and managed-secret rewrap counts, old/new secret acceptance counts, dependency credential review, readiness, alerting, warning codes, and redaction booleans only; it does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, secret refs, secret values, tokens, API keys, key material, webhook signing secrets, or raw log lines. Compose, Helm values/schema, OpenAPI, TypeScript SDK, and operator docs now expose the contract. Product completion still requires executing the selected target secret-rotation drill and mounting reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest migration drill backend posture work adds metadata-only failed-migration drill evidence support without changing the database. Operators can generate reviewed `romeo.migration-drill-evidence.v1` with `pnpm evidence:migration-drill`, mount it through `MIGRATION_DRILL_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and read sanitized posture through `GET /api/v1/admin/migrations/drill-posture` plus `client.admin.migrationDrillPosture()`. The report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, migration counts, fail-closed migration-job posture, blocked cutover posture, rollback/retry recovery, schema/app validation, runbook review, warning codes, and redaction booleans only; it does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, database URLs, migration SQL, migration logs, raw error stacks, or secret values. Compose, Helm values/schema, OpenAPI, TypeScript SDK, and operator docs now expose the contract. Product completion still requires executing the selected target failed-migration drill and mounting reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest provider outage backend posture work adds metadata-only target provider outage evidence support without changing the database. Operators can generate reviewed `romeo.provider-outage-evidence.v1` with `pnpm evidence:provider-outage`, mount it through `PROVIDER_OUTAGE_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and read sanitized posture through `GET /api/v1/admin/providers/outage-posture` plus `client.admin.providerOutagePosture()`. The report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, outage counts, runtime circuit/fallback/kill-switch counts, provider operational-summary readback, alerting, recovery, warning codes, and redaction booleans only; it does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, provider endpoints, raw provider payloads, provider responses, provider errors, prompts, API keys, alert payloads, or secret values. Compose, Helm values/schema, OpenAPI, TypeScript SDK, generated Python SDK, and operator docs now expose the contract. Validation passed script syntax check, `pnpm evidence:provider-outage -- --output dist/ci/provider-outage-evidence.json`, focused `@romeo/config`, `@romeo/core`, and `@romeo/api-client` type checks/tests, OpenAPI route coverage, OpenAPI export, Python SDK regeneration at 334 operations plus drift check, docs command check, Compose env contract smoke, Helm env-schema contract smoke, Kubernetes render smoke, strict greenfield baseline review, regenerated GA checklist/preflight/target-plan evidence, broad `pnpm check`, and no-op `@romeo/db db:generate`. The draft checklist remains 19 of 29 with the same ten live target-environment blockers, and no migration files were added.

Latest target resilience GA hardening adds an optional strict Phase 34 gate for provider outage, failed-migration, network-partition, and secret-rotation target drills without schema churn. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-target-resilience-drills` / `GA_REQUIRE_TARGET_RESILIENCE_DRILLS=true` now requires passed live metadata-only `romeo.provider-outage-evidence.v1`, `romeo.migration-drill-evidence.v1`, `romeo.network-partition-evidence.v1`, and `romeo.secret-rotation-drill-evidence.v1` files, validates each drill's required checks, runtime/recovery/readback counts, failure-code posture, and redaction flags, and marks the combined gate security-critical and environment-required. `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps the gate to the four safe evidence-generation commands and requires `PROVIDER_OUTAGE_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true`, `MIGRATION_DRILL_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true`, `NETWORK_PARTITION_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true`, and `SECRET_ROTATION_DRILL_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true` before reporting ready. Validation passed script syntax checks, generated sample evidence for all four drill files, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-target-resilience-drills.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:target-preflight-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-contract-target-resilience-drills.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-target-resilience-drills --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-target-resilience-drills.json`, `pnpm ga:target-preflight -- --checklist dist/ci/ga-checklist-target-resilience-drills.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-target-resilience-drills.json`, and `pnpm ga:target-plan -- --preflight dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-target-resilience-drills.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-plan-target-resilience-drills.json`. The optional gate reports satisfied when reviewed evidence is present; the checklist remains blocked only by the same ten target-environment gates, and no migration files were added.

Latest billing operations GA hardening adds an optional strict Phase 33 gate for target billing worker cadence, entitlement/lifecycle API readback, worker-log redaction, and alerting evidence without schema churn. `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-billing-operations-live` / `GA_REQUIRE_BILLING_OPERATIONS_LIVE=true` now requires passed live metadata-only `romeo.billing-operations-evidence.v1`, validates required checks, cadence counts, entitlement/lifecycle worker success, API readback, alert posture, failure-code absence, and redaction flags, and marks the gate security-critical and environment-required. `pnpm ga:target-preflight` maps the gate to `BILLING_OPERATIONS_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true pnpm evidence:billing-operations -- --output dist/ci/billing-operations-evidence.json` and requires reviewed-evidence confirmation before reporting ready. Validation passed script syntax checks, `pnpm evidence:billing-operations -- --output dist/ci/billing-operations-evidence.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-billing-operations-live.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:target-preflight-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-contract-billing-operations-live.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --require-billing-operations-live --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-billing-operations-live.json`, `BILLING_OPERATIONS_EVIDENCE_REVIEWED=true pnpm ga:target-preflight -- --checklist dist/ci/ga-checklist-billing-operations-live.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-billing-operations-live.json`, `pnpm ga:target-plan -- --preflight dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-billing-operations-live.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-plan-billing-operations-live.json`, docs command check, strict greenfield baseline review, focused Prettier, and migration inventory review. The optional gate reports satisfied when reviewed evidence is present; the checklist reports 20 of 30 gates satisfied with the same ten live target-environment blockers, product completion still requires executing the selected target billing CronJobs and alert drills before recording reviewed evidence, and no migration files were added.

Latest billing operations backend posture work adds metadata-only target billing cadence/readback/alert evidence support without changing the database. Operators can generate reviewed `romeo.billing-operations-evidence.v1` with `pnpm evidence:billing-operations`, mount it through `BILLING_OPERATIONS_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and read sanitized posture through `GET /api/v1/admin/billing/operations-posture` plus `client.admin.billingOperationsPosture()`. The report returns evidence status, required-check coverage, cadence counts, entitlement/lifecycle worker summaries, API readback, alerting, warning codes, and redaction booleans only; it does not return mounted paths, evidence bodies, raw billing provider payloads, worker logs, API keys, customer identifiers, alert payloads, or secret values. Compose, Helm values/schema, OpenAPI, TypeScript SDK, generated Python SDK, and operator docs now expose the contract. Latest Kubernetes app-image immutability hardening aligns live Kubernetes evidence collection with the enterprise prebuilt-image path. `pnpm ga:target-preflight` no longer requires Docker for the target Kubernetes live-smoke or Kubernetes DR gates; it requires `kubectl`/`helm`, `KUBERNETES_LIVE_SMOKE_SKIP_BUILD=true`, a reviewed digest-pinned `KUBERNETES_LIVE_SMOKE_APP_IMAGE`, digest-pinned live-smoke dependency images, `KUBERNETES_DR_SKIP_BUILD=true`, and a reviewed digest-pinned `KUBERNETES_DR_APP_IMAGE` before marking the live or DR gates ready. The generated live and DR commands pass `--skip-build --image` from the selected env vars while still validating namespaces, the shared DR plan, and CloudNativePG Secret presence without returning image refs, Secret names, keys, values, database URLs, raw command output, or evidence bodies. Latest CI release-evidence hardening wires the GA evidence bundle and release approval contract into hosted CI dry-run coverage. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` now generates dry-run release approval evidence after release provenance, requires that approval in `pnpm release:publish-plan`, generates a release GA checklist draft, generates `romeo.ga-evidence-bundle.v1`, and requires both approval and GA-bundle linkage in `pnpm release:airgap-check`. `pnpm ci:branch-protection-plan` now requires `pnpm release:approval` and `pnpm ga:bundle` in the workflow command set so hosted CI readback cannot silently drop the promotion manifest path. Validation passed script syntax checks, focused Prettier, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json`, `pnpm docs:check-commands -- --output dist/ci/docs-command-check-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json`, `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output dist/ci/greenfield-baseline-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json`, `pnpm ga:target-preflight -- --checklist dist/ci/ga-checklist-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-kubernetes-live-auth-redaction.json`, and a secret-pattern redaction scan across the generated live Kubernetes hardening artifacts. Latest Kubernetes load/soak preflight hardening makes the selected-tier scale gate match the GA validator. `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:load-soak` now accepts `KUBERNETES_LOAD_SOAK_*` env equivalents for tier, iterations, soak seconds, interval, timeout, namespace, release, service, deployment, selector, service port, and optional ingress base URL. The GA validator now rejects load/soak evidence whose requested soak is under 60 seconds, and `pnpm ga:target-preflight` blocks the gate unless the operator configures a `small` or `enterprise` tier, at least two iterations, at least 60 requested soak seconds, bounded interval/timeout values, target namespace/app Deployment/app Service posture, and safe base-URL posture when supplied. The preflight evidence returns only tier, iteration, duration, and safe target posture; it does not return API keys, selector strings, fixture payloads, pod logs, or load evidence bodies. Validation passed focused syntax checks, env-driven load/soak dry-run evidence at `dist/ci/kubernetes-load-soak-plan-preflight-env.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:target-preflight-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-contract-load-soak-preflight.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-load-soak-duration.json`, `pnpm docs:check-commands -- --output dist/ci/docs-command-check-load-soak-preflight.json`, `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output dist/ci/greenfield-baseline-load-soak-preflight.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-load-soak-preflight.json`, `pnpm ga:target-preflight -- --checklist dist/ci/ga-checklist-load-soak-preflight.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-load-soak-preflight.json`, and a redaction scan over the new load/soak preflight artifacts. The draft checklist remains blocked by the same ten live target-environment gates, and no migration files were added.

Latest Kubernetes live-smoke preflight hardening makes the Phase 21 namespace smoke safer for enterprise target collection. `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:live` now honors `KUBERNETES_LIVE_SMOKE_*` env equivalents for namespace, release, app image, smoke-owned Postgres/Valkey/RustFS/object-store-client images, skip-build, and timeout, and it now actually writes `--output` evidence. The live evidence includes metadata-only image posture, and the GA validator rejects live-smoke evidence unless the app image was reviewed and dependency images are digest-pinned. `pnpm ga:target-preflight` now blocks the gate unless `KUBERNETES_LIVE_SMOKE_SKIP_BUILD=true`, a reviewed digest-pinned app image is configured, the smoke dependency images are digest-pinned, timeout is bounded, and the selected smoke namespace is free. The preflight evidence returns only namespace/release names, image counts, and posture booleans; it does not return dependency image refs, generated secrets, pod logs, or evidence bodies. Validation passed focused syntax checks, `pnpm smoke:ga:target-preflight-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-contract-kubernetes-live-preflight.json`, `pnpm smoke:ga:evidence-contract -- --output dist/ci/ga-evidence-contract-kubernetes-live-images.json`, `pnpm docs:check-commands -- --output dist/ci/docs-command-check-kubernetes-live-preflight.json`, `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output dist/ci/greenfield-baseline-kubernetes-live-preflight.json`, `pnpm ga:checklist -- --output dist/ci/ga-checklist-kubernetes-live-preflight.json`, `pnpm ga:target-preflight -- --checklist dist/ci/ga-checklist-kubernetes-live-preflight.json --output dist/ci/ga-target-preflight-kubernetes-live-preflight.json`, and a redaction scan over the new live-smoke artifacts. The draft checklist remains blocked by the same ten live target-environment gates, and no migration files were added.

Latest suspended-tenant backend hardening closes the Phase 33 workflow execution gap and extends the same boundary to direct model/eval/voice, scheduled work-definition, browser artifact upload, webhook egress, and notification retry paths. The org abuse-control policy already blocked new model runs, tool execution/dispatch, connector sync, worker enqueue, knowledge ingestion, and file-upload creation; workflow run start, approval, resume, direct OpenAI-compatible chat/embedding requests, eval provider runs, voice provider calls, scheduled connector/workflow creation, browser automation artifact upload registration, explicit webhook test/retry egress, and explicit notification retry egress now call the same shared enforcement boundary before advancing work. Latest backend/storage work closes the local pgvector physical-partition DDL gap without creating a migration chain. The single greenfield baseline now creates `knowledge_chunk_embeddings` as a 16-way hash-partitioned table on `org_id`, uses a partition-safe composite primary key, and includes `org_id` in the embedding chunk/provider/model uniqueness contract used by repository upserts. Validation passed focused Prettier, `pnpm --filter @romeo/db db:generate` with no schema changes after folding the metadata into `0000_snapshot.json`, focused DB schema/vector tests, `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output dist/ci/greenfield-baseline-pgvector-partitioned.json`, a disposable pgvector Postgres migration apply through `pnpm migrate:postgres`, live `pnpm validate:postgres -- --output dist/ci/postgres-schema-pgvector-partitioned.json`, and live `pnpm review:pgvector-isolation -- --output dist/ci/pgvector-isolation-local-baseline.json`. The migration inventory remains only the locked greenfield baseline plus Drizzle metadata. Target-deployment pgvector claims still require running `pnpm review:pgvector-isolation` against the selected database and mounting that evidence before setting `VECTOR_ISOLATION_MODE=pgvector_partitioned_by_org`.

Latest CI/Postgres evidence hardening wires the live pgvector physical-isolation review into the Postgres Conformance job, immediately after migration, schema validation, and query-plan review. The Kubernetes render smoke now also asserts that Helm renders `VECTOR_ISOLATION_MODE`, `PGVECTOR_PHYSICAL_ISOLATION_EVIDENCE_PATH`, and `QDRANT_LIVE_EVIDENCE_PATH` through the app ConfigMap with safe defaults. ## Implemented Findings

- Postgres schema is split by domain under `packages/db/src/schema`; the validator currently tracks 73 required tables plus explicit indexes.
- Kubernetes DR evidence now has a live harness through `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:dr`. External-Postgres mode provisions disposable source/restore pgvector dependencies and CloudNativePG mode consumes operator-managed source/restore `DATABASE_URL` Secrets, while both modes run the shared Postgres/object-store backup, restore, schema-validation, readiness, API readback, attachment readback, and pod-log redaction path. Live artifacts remain pending for `dist/ci/kubernetes-external-postgres-dr.json` and `dist/ci/kubernetes-cloudnativepg-dr.json`.
- Kubernetes NetworkPolicy enforcement now has a guarded live harness through `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:networkpolicy`. It creates an ephemeral namespace, proves baseline client reachability, applies an app-labeled egress policy, requires allowed egress to remain reachable and denied egress to fail, and scans pod logs for a generated sentinel. Dry-run output is planning evidence only; live CNI evidence remains pending until run against an accepted cluster.
- Kubernetes worker evidence now has a guarded live harness through `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:workers`. It targets an existing Helm release with core worker CronJobs enabled, verifies the worker API-key Secret/key, creates controlled API fixtures, starts Jobs from rendered CronJobs for data connector sync, workflow resume, webhook retry, notification retry, retention enforcement, billing entitlement reconciliation, and billing lifecycle enforcement, validates metadata-only JSON output, force-deletes a running workflow-resume pod after the first loop iteration, reruns workflow-resume, and proves no duplicate linked run or workflow run was created. Optional Valkey-backed quota coordination now exists through `QUOTA_COORDINATION_DRIVER=valkey`, `GET /api/v1/quotas/distributed-status`, and TypeScript SDK `client.admin.quotaCoordinationStatus()`; it atomically reserves matching quota buckets before Postgres writes, syncs admin quota mutations, reports sanitized health without `VALKEY_URL`, and fails closed when configured coordination is unavailable. Edge security posture now exists through `GET /api/v1/admin/edge-security/posture` and `client.admin.edgeSecurityPosture()`, reporting sanitized TLS termination, trusted-proxy, WAF, allowed-origin count, HSTS, request body limit, native file/direct-upload/resumable-upload/chat-attachment size limits, HTTP rate-limit driver/window/max posture, and defensive header posture without raw origins, ingress annotations, proxy IP ranges, raw client identifiers, object-store keys, or secrets; the API emits defensive security headers, rejects request bodies above `REQUEST_BODY_MAX_BYTES`, enforces configured file and attachment size limits, enforces app-level public/auth/webhook/authenticated request-rate limits with sanitized 429 responses, and Compose/Helm expose the matching env controls. `pnpm smoke:compose:distributed-controls` now provides local live two-replica proof for shared Valkey-backed HTTP counters, quota coordination health, cross-replica quota consumption, and fail-closed quota behavior during Valkey outage. Scheduled reconciliation now has `romeo workers billing-entitlement-reconcile`, a Compose `billing-entitlement-reconcile-worker`, and Helm `workers.billingEntitlementReconcile` CronJob wiring. Billing lifecycle automation now exists through `GET /api/v1/billing/lifecycle`, `POST /api/v1/billing/lifecycle/enforce`, TypeScript SDK `client.admin.billingLifecycle()` / `client.admin.enforceBillingLifecycle()`, generated Python SDK operations, `romeo billing lifecycle`, `romeo billing enforce-lifecycle`, `romeo workers billing-lifecycle-enforce`, Compose `billing-lifecycle-enforce-worker`, and Helm `workers.billingLifecycleEnforce`; it stores only bounded lifecycle timestamps in billing-plan metadata and enforces due trial/subscription/cancel/grace transitions without raw provider payloads. `pnpm smoke:compose:billing-scheduler` now provides local live worker evidence for scheduled billing reconciliation and lifecycle enforcement by repairing deliberate quota drift, marking an expired trial `past_due`, and scanning worker/Compose logs for generated secrets and raw billing sentinel content. - Local password authentication now has durable Argon2id password credentials with legacy scrypt verification/rehash compatibility, encrypted TOTP MFA factors, one-time local MFA recovery codes stored only as encrypted salted-hash envelopes, short-lived MFA challenge tokens, user self-service password/MFA APIs, self-service email/name profile updates through `PATCH /api/v1/me`, admin password reset APIs, and local login paths that mint the normal hashed `rms_` session cookie. Local session cookies are HttpOnly, `SameSite=Lax`, revocable, and now protected by CSRF origin/fetch-metadata checks for unsafe browser mutations against `APP_ORIGIN` and `EDGE_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, with signed SAML and billing webhook callback paths relying on their own replay/signature checks. `GET /api/v1/me` now returns the current subject email and display name for profile editors. Self-service email changes are normalized, same-org conflict checked, synced into local password credential lookup, and audited without raw email/name values. Known-user local login failures now write metadata-only `local_auth.login.failure` audit events with stable failure classes for invalid password, credential lockout, disabled user, invalid MFA code, invalid recovery code, and missing MFA factor; unknown-email failures return the same generic login error while writing `unknown_principal` audit events through a disabled per-org `system_local_auth` actor with keyed HMAC identifier hashes instead of raw emails. Local MFA enrollment, confirmation, recovery-code generation/use, and disable actions write metadata-only factor-type/count audit events, and focused tests prove passwords, TOTP codes, TOTP secrets, raw recovery codes, factor display names, and emails stay out of audit metadata. Signed billing webhook audits now also use disabled per-org `system_billing_webhook` actors so public webhook audit writes satisfy the Postgres audit foreign-key contract without creating login-capable principals.
- After adding CSRF origin/fetch-metadata protection, focused `@romeo/core` checks and API tests passed; `pnpm --filter @romeo/db db:generate` reported no schema changes; `pnpm docs:check-commands -- --output dist/ci/docs-command-check-csrf-protection.json` passed; and broad `pnpm lint`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, and `pnpm build` passed. The guard rejects cross-site browser mutations without logging request bodies or cookie values, while configured browser origins and signed callback/webhook paths continue to work.
- Org/global admin roles are persisted on users, promoted through `PATCH /api/v1/users/{userId}/role`, reflected in authenticated subjects, and role changes revoke target sessions/API keys. Global-admin grants require an existing global admin and cannot be self-mutated.
- The enterprise auth-provider catalog is available through `GET /api/v1/admin/auth-providers/catalog`, persisted provider policy is available through `GET/PATCH /api/v1/admin/auth-providers/settings`, and provider-card connection tests are available through `POST /api/v1/admin/auth-providers/settings/test` for the frontend app-store-style page. Local auth, generic OIDC, Keycloak, Google, Azure AD/Entra ID, Okta, Auth0, GitHub direct OAuth2, LDAP, Active Directory, and SAML are marked implemented. Provider settings persist through `system_settings`, support global defaults plus sparse org overrides, expose inherited/effective state, store per-provider OIDC issuer/client/group mapping config for implemented OIDC providers, store sanitized GitHub OAuth2 policy with managed `secretRef` pointers, store sanitized LDAP/AD bind/search policy with managed bind-secret refs, store sanitized SAML SP/IdP policy with managed IdP certificate refs, sanitize secret refs, and require explicit confirmation before disabling local fallback. `GET /api/v1/auth/oidc/start?providerId=...&orgId=...` starts browser PKCE login against the selected global or org-scoped OIDC provider config, and the callback uses signed state plus `openid-client` authorization-code grant processing and Romeo JWT claim mapping, allowing multiple live OIDC IdPs with distinct issuers/client IDs selected per org. `GET /api/v1/auth/oauth2/start?providerId=github` starts GitHub direct OAuth2 login with Authorization Code + PKCE, verified email lookup, optional allowed-domain/org/team/admin-team policy, durable mapped-group sync for known Romeo groups, and metadata-only audit. `POST /api/v1/auth/ldap/login` authenticates LDAP/Active Directory users with LDAPS or StartTLS-required LDAP, bounded service bind/search, user bind verification, optional allowed-domain/required-group/admin-group policy, durable mapped-group sync for known Romeo groups, and metadata-only audit. `GET /api/v1/auth/saml/start`, `POST /api/v1/auth/saml/callback`, and `GET /api/v1/auth/saml/metadata` provide package-backed SAML login, signed assertion validation, bounded replay protection, mapped-group sync for known Romeo groups, admin-group promotion, and metadata-only audit. Provider-card tests reuse sanitized OIDC discovery/JWKS checks, GitHub OAuth2 metadata/secret/API reachability checks, LDAP service-bind/base-search checks, or SAML configuration/certificate-ref/endpoint-readiness checks and do not return client IDs, full issuer paths, secret refs, tokens, provider account IDs, GitHub org/team names, LDAP URLs, bind DNs, search filters, directory entries, SAML entry points, SAML entity IDs, SAML assertions, SAML certificates, SAML group names, or JWKS URLs.

- Guarded destructive directory lifecycle sync is now implemented through `POST /api/v1/admin/directory-sync`, `client.admin.directorySync(...)`, OpenAPI, and generated Python SDK coverage. Admins can preview by default or apply only with `confirmApply: "apply-directory-sync"`; the service can disable users missing from a supplied directory inventory and remove stale group memberships, with caps, current-caller preservation, admin-preservation defaults, explicit admin-disable opt-in, credential revocation through the existing user-disable lifecycle, and metadata-only audit. Responses include internal IDs, counts, warnings, and redaction flags, but exclude user emails, names, external subject IDs, external group names, raw directory payloads, and reason text. `pnpm smoke:directory-sync:contract` now writes reusable local evidence for unauthenticated denial, preview/apply guardrails, cap enforcement, admin/current-caller preservation, stale membership cleanup, disabled-user credential revocation, and audit/evidence redaction. The slice uses existing user, group, membership, credential, and audit tables and adds no migration files. Org admins keep same-org admin behavior, but cross-org resource/list access requires `global_admin`; focused authorization tests cover guessed-workspace cross-org denial for chat, run, agent, knowledge-base, and service-account surfaces, plus explicit global-admin override behavior. This is a backend security hardening change only and adds no schema or migration files.
- OpenWebUI-reference channel message backend support remains available without adding migration files, but it is now an explicit opt-in bridge behind `OPENWEBUI_COMPATIBILITY_ENABLED=true`, not the default Romeo product surface. The default OpenAPI/Python SDK surface omits these operations and bridge-only component schemas; the opt-in OpenAPI route coverage mode still proves the full bridge route set has matching OpenAPI operations. Native Socket.IO/WebSocket transport compatibility remains future bridge work.

- Native Romeo file/object storage is implemented without adding migration files. The backend exposes `GET /api/v1/files`, bounded inline `POST /api/v1/files`, direct-upload `POST /api/v1/files/uploads`, resumable-upload `POST /api/v1/files/uploads/resumable`, `GET /api/v1/files/uploads/resumable/{fileId}`, `POST /api/v1/files/uploads/resumable/{fileId}/complete`, `DELETE /api/v1/files/uploads/resumable/{fileId}`, the direct-upload refresh/complete/cancel paths, `GET /api/v1/files/{fileId}`, `GET/POST /api/v1/files/{fileId}/shares`, `GET /api/v1/files/{fileId}/content`, and `DELETE /api/v1/files/{fileId}`. Inline uploads are bounded base64 payloads and require allowlisted MIME plus signature-matching bytes; direct-upload sessions return short-lived object-store PUT URLs, require declared size plus SHA-256, deny content readback while `status=uploading`, and verify stored size, digest, and MIME signature before marking metadata `available`. Resumable sessions return short-lived object-store PUT URLs for bounded parts, store only part count/part size/mode metadata, compose the final object server-side, verify part sizes plus final size/digest/MIME signature, delete staged parts, and then mark metadata `available`. Chat image attachments also require PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP bytes matching the declared MIME signature before object-store persistence. Metadata persists in the greenfield `object_records` table with org/workspace/owner fields, purpose, status, size, MIME type, checksum, lifecycle timestamps, and metadata JSON. Public responses include server-issued content URLs and omit raw object keys. Owner read/write grants are created by default, explicit file sharing uses the standard `ResourceGrant` contract with file `read`/`write` permissions, file reads/deletes enforce `files:read`/`files:write` plus object grants, delete removes stored bytes and staged parts before marking metadata deleted, and audits store only purpose, size, MIME type, workspace metadata, part counts/sizes, sharing principal type/permission categories, and upload lifecycle action category.

The helper rejects spoofed PNG/PDF/GIF declarations without returning raw bytes, object keys, or content samples.

- Auth-provider secret ingestion is now available through `POST /api/v1/admin/secrets`, `client.createManagedSecret(...)`, and `client.admin.createManagedSecret(...)`. The endpoint accepts a one-time value and defaults to storing an AES-GCM encrypted envelope in the existing `system_settings` table using `MANAGED_SECRET_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, returning only a `romeo-secret://...` ref. It also accepts `storageDriver: "vault"` with an optional `targetSecretRef` to write the value to Vault KV-v2 through configured `VAULT_*` settings and return a `vault://...` ref without storing the raw value locally. Settings, readiness, and audits stay on scheme/driver/count/provider metadata only, and externally managed refs (`env://`, `vault://`, `external-secret://`, `aws-sm://`, `gcp-sm://`, `azure-kv://`) remain supported without schema churn.
- Staged local MFA and locally managed-secret envelope rewrap is now available through `POST /api/v1/admin/secret-rotation/rewrap/preview`, `POST /api/v1/admin/secret-rotation/rewrap`, `client.admin.previewSecretRewrap(...)`, and `client.admin.executeSecretRewrap(...)`. The backend supports `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS` and `MANAGED_SECRET_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS` during short rotation windows, rewrites local MFA and local `romeo-secret://` envelopes with the active keys, gates global managed-secret envelopes to global admins, requires explicit execute confirmation, and returns/audits only counts, failure codes, scope posture, and redaction flags without TOTP secrets, recovery-code values, secret refs, secret values, user emails, or key material. No schema migration was added.
- Auth-provider setting updates now write metadata-only `admin.auth_provider_settings.update` audit summaries. The audit metadata records changed global/org scopes, changed provider IDs, enable/disable, display/login-order/domain/org-policy/disabled-reason/OIDC-connection/OIDC-mapping/secret-ref change categories, provider enablement counts, secret-ref posture counts, and local-fallback posture without raw display names, domains, issuer URLs, client IDs, secret refs, or secret values.
- Runtime tenancy mode is now explicit through `TENANCY_MODE=single|multi` and `GET /api/v1/me` returns `deployment.tenancyMode`. The default is `single` for Compose/self-hosted installs; frontends can hide org/global scope switchers unless the deployment is configured as `multi`. OpenAPI now documents the raw `/me` bootstrap shape with `BootstrapResponse`, including `deployment.tenancyMode`, so generated clients do not have to infer it from source.
- API bootstrap and service-account persistence are now aligned for frontend and automation work. `GET /api/v1/me` is the raw bootstrap response, exposes `subject.email`, `subject.name`, optional `user`, and `deployment.tenancyMode`, and the TypeScript SDK/OpenAPI contract matches that shape. Service-account-created chats, agents, knowledge bases/sources, runs, workflows, connectors, webhooks, evals, tool calls, usage, and audit events now persist through disabled per-purpose system actors so FK-backed ledgers stay durable without turning service accounts into login-capable users.
- Native-client device refresh is now usable in secure mode through the public `POST /api/v1/device-authorizations/refresh` route. It accepts only the refresh token, stays in the auth-sensitive pre-auth rate-limit bucket, rotates the backing API key plus refresh token, stores refresh tokens only as hashes, and focused regression coverage proves old access-token denial plus new access-token `/me` readback with `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`.
- Tool connector type posture is now exposed through metadata-only `GET /api/v1/tool-connectors/catalog`, OpenAPI, and `client.tool.catalog()`. The catalog reports built-in tools, imported OpenAPI operations, single-operation webhook tool connectors, and HTTPS Streamable HTTP MCP manifest connectors as implemented, browser automation as a separate workflow API, and enterprise tool connector types as planned with stable blocker codes until reviewed out-of-process runtimes exist. Webhook tool connector creation is available through `POST /api/v1/tools/webhook` and `client.tool.createWebhookConnector(...)`; it validates an HTTPS target without query-string secrets, creates a disabled `webhook` connector plus disabled POST operation, derives a host allowlist, and still requires the existing activation, approval, model-tool, worker, and egress gates before execution. MCP connector creation is available through `POST /api/v1/tools/mcp` and `client.tool.createMcpConnector(...)`; it accepts a reviewed static manifest for an HTTPS Streamable HTTP endpoint, rejects unsafe/local/private server URLs and query-string secrets, creates disabled connector/operation records, derives a host allowlist, and sends execution through the same worker boundary. The catalog returns only capability, scope, security-control, and redaction flags, without raw connector configs, endpoint URLs, secret refs, or secret values.
- Phase 27 connector intake now has a metadata-only backend catalog through `GET /api/v1/data-connectors/catalog`, `client.dataConnectors.catalog()`, OpenAPI, and the generated Python SDK. The catalog covers all supported connector types, typed config keys, sync mode, execution boundary, credential source categories, runtime driver posture, egress posture, fetch timeout/byte/retry limits, stable blocked reasons, and credential posture booleans without allowed-host values, endpoint URLs, secret refs, tokens, or credentials. New connector types must be added to `packages/core/src/domain/data-connector-catalog.ts` before creation logic can accept them, and managed connector creation now rejects runtime-blocked types with `409 connector_runtime_not_configured` plus stable blocked reason codes rather than persisting unsyncable outbound connectors. [Data Connector Intake](../data-connector-intake.md) records the required threat-model, adapter, redaction, deployment, and deletion evidence. Website/RSS/Atlassian/Notion/Linear/Slack managed fetch now performs production DNS lookup before fetch and rejects DNS-resolved private/link-local/reserved hosts before network egress. Managed website/RSS, GitHub, S3, Confluence, Jira, Notion, Linear, and Slack fetches retry only bounded transient response classes, including rate limits, with shared retry/backoff config surfaced in Compose, Helm, the catalog API, and generated clients. Confluence/Jira, Notion, Linear, and Slack use connector secret refs only for their SaaS credentials, and sync summaries return query/channel hashes rather than raw query strings or channel IDs.

- Notification delivery retry is now implemented through `POST /api/v1/notification-deliveries/retry-due`, `client.notifications.retryDue()`, generated Python SDK `retry_due_notifications()`, `romeo notifications retry-due`, and the deployable `romeo workers notification-retry` loop. Transient webhook, Resend-compatible email, SMTP email, Slack-compatible, Teams-compatible, PagerDuty Events API, and FCM mobile-push notification delivery failures schedule metadata-only retry state; successful retries clear stale retry/error fields; exhausted attempts write metadata-only dead-letter details. `NOTIFICATION_DELIVERY_DRIVER=configured` can route mixed channel types through one deployment, with `NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_DELIVERY_DRIVER=resend|smtp` selecting the email adapter; single-adapter driver modes remain available. - Notification delivery policy is now implemented through `GET/PATCH /api/v1/admin/notification-policy`, `client.notifications.policy()/updatePolicy(...)`, generated Python SDK `notification_policy()` / `update_notification_policy(...)`, and `romeo notifications policy/policy-update`. Registered artifacts are written through the configured object-store abstraction and read back through server-issued `/api/v1/browser-automation-artifacts/{artifactId}` URLs with org/workspace authorization, while normal workflow output omits object-store keys. Retention enforcement now deletes stale registered browser artifacts from terminal jobs and strips their job metadata with aggregate counts only. `romeo workers browser-automation` delegates execution to a configured HTTPS runner URL, accepts bounded metadata-only completion results, and keeps raw task text out of worker stdout, stderr, and normal readback. `pnpm smoke:browser-automation:contract` now provides local backend/API evidence for approved-task metadata redaction, stale running-task reclaim at attempt 2, external runner completion, registered artifact readback, object-store cleanup through retention enforcement, and redacted retention audit/readback evidence. - Org RAG policy is now implemented through `GET/PATCH /api/v1/admin/rag/policy`, `client.admin.ragPolicy(...)`, `client.admin.updateRagPolicy(...)`, OpenAPI `RagPolicyReport` / `UpdateRagPolicyRequest`, and generated Python SDK operations. Data residency tags plus explicit external-vector namespace, partitioning, physical vector isolation mode/enforcement, Postgres-authoritative DR/reindex strategy, metadata-only export policy, and restore-validation posture are now stored and audited as stable metadata only.
