- Voice profile persistence now has provider-voice natural-key protection, list/enabled lookup indexes, and `updated_at` lifecycle tracking for catalog upserts.
- Generated voice artifacts now return only server-issued `playbackUrl`/`deleteUrl` values plus redaction posture, never raw object-store keys. `DELETE /api/v1/voice-artifacts/{artifactId}` and `client.voice.deleteArtifact(...)` remove owner/admin-authorized artifacts, redact the backing usage-event metadata to a storage-key hash, and audit only metadata-only deletion posture.
- Governed chat deletion now covers messages, message parts, runs, run steps/events, comments, notifications/deliveries, run-linked tool traces, related usage events, grants, workspace folder items, and defensive direct chat favorite cleanup. Active legal holds are guarded at the service and Postgres repository boundary.
- Chat history lifecycle APIs now cover active/archived/all list filters, title rename, archive, unarchive, legal hold, same-workspace message-prefix fork/branch creation, assistant-message feedback/rating state, chat favorites, and governed deletion cleanup parity across in-memory and Postgres repository paths without adding a migration.
- The app server selects the reusable Postgres repository factory behind `REPOSITORY_DRIVER=postgres`; `REPOSITORY_DRIVER=memory` remains development-only and is rejected when `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`.
- The Phase 19 baseline migration is locked at `packages/db/migrations/0000_greenfield_baseline.sql` and explicitly enables pgvector before creating vector-backed embedding tables.
- The baseline candidate was live-applied through `pnpm migrate:postgres` to disposable pgvector containers and passed `pnpm validate:postgres`; static review now passes with shortened foreign-key names under PostgreSQL's identifier limit.
- The shared `pnpm migrate:postgres` command delegates to the database package migrator and is wired into the Compose `migrate` service.
- `pnpm seed:postgres -- --confirm-development-seed` now provides an explicit idempotent development/bootstrap seed for local smoke paths. It is not part of migration and refuses to run without the confirmation flag.
- Compose app startup now waits for the migration service, object-store bucket bootstrap, and Valkey health, exposes a container health check against `/api/v1/health`, and has an env example for replacing development defaults before shared use.
- Compose now mounts the pgvector/Postgres 18 data volume at `/var/lib/postgresql`, matching the PostgreSQL 18 container layout, and the RustFS bucket bootstrap uses an explicit shell entrypoint so the `mc` command is not misinterpreted.
- Compose now defines explicit profiles for data connector sync, workflow resume, webhook retry, notification retry, retention enforcement, tool dispatch, knowledge extraction, voice catalog sync, and Postgres backup; the runtime image includes `pdftotext` and a Postgres 18 client for optional extraction and backup operations.
- `pnpm smoke:compose` now runs a clean temporary Compose project with randomized host ports and generated high-entropy secrets, applies migrations, runs the explicit seed, creates a real API key, recreates the app with `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`, verifies unauthenticated API denial, verifies admin readiness is `ready`, writes chat, run, usage, audit, notification, delivery, knowledge-source, attachment, and webhook-delivery records through Postgres/RustFS, restarts the app, restarts Valkey, RustFS, and Postgres one service at a time, re-reads durable records, redacted webhook delivery payload metadata, and attachment bytes after each restart, runs schema validation inside the container, scans captured Compose logs for generated secrets plus raw prompt/comment/document/webhook sentinels, and tears the project down.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:workers` now runs a clean temporary Compose project with randomized host ports and generated high-entropy secrets, applies migrations, runs the explicit seed, creates a real API key, recreates the app with `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`, verifies readiness, creates and publishes a smoke-owned agent, runs data connector sync, workflow resume, webhook retry, notification retry, retention enforcement, knowledge extraction, and voice catalog sync workers once through their real CLI commands, validates JSON output, starts and restarts workflow-resume, webhook-retry, and notification-retry loop-worker services with short test intervals, records `loopRestarts` evidence, verifies workflow-resume handles controlled pending work with raw prompt sentinels redacted from output, SIGKILLs and restarts the workflow-resume service during controlled pending work, and confirms recovery reaches `waiting_approval` without duplicate linked model runs or workflow runs.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:backup-restore` now runs a populated source Compose project, writes a chat attachment through the run API, writes Postgres and object-store backups plus manifests through the Compose backup profile, restores both into an isolated second Compose project through `pnpm drill:postgres-restore` and `pnpm drill:object-store-restore`, validates the restored schema, starts the restored app with seeded login disabled, verifies readiness, re-reads restored chat, knowledge, query results, and attachment bytes through the API, and scans source/restore Compose logs for generated secrets.
- Postgres backup upload PUTs are now bounded by `POSTGRES_BACKUP_UPLOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` / `--upload-timeout-ms`, defaulting to `30000`, and Compose/Helm backup jobs pass the value explicitly while keeping presigned URLs secret-backed and redacted in dry-run plans. `pnpm smoke:postgres:backup-upload-failure` proves HTTP failure and connected slow-upload timeout paths exit nonzero, do not write success manifests, and redact presigned URL secrets from output.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:object-store-outage` now runs live Compose RustFS outage evidence by starting a secure temporary stack, writing and reading a baseline attachment, stopping RustFS, verifying attachment read and write fail safely without persisting the failed write or echoing raw content, restarting RustFS, verifying the baseline attachment remains readable, writing a recovery attachment, and scanning Compose logs for generated secrets plus raw outage sentinels.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:external-postgres` now runs the Compose app against an external pgvector/PostgreSQL 18 container instead of the bundled Compose Postgres service. It applies the same migration command through `DATABASE_URL`, runs the explicit seed, recreates the app with `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`, verifies readiness, runs durable product workflow readback after restart, validates schema, confirms the override renders without the bundled `postgres` service, and scans captured logs for generated database/object-store/session/webhook/API secrets. The external override also resets the optional `postgres-backup` dependency on the bundled service so backup-profile config renders against the supplied external `DATABASE_URL`.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:secret-rotation` now runs live Compose rotation evidence for `SESSION_SECRET`, `SESSION_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, and `WEBHOOK_SIGNING_KEY`. It starts from a secure-mode Compose install, creates a real admin API key and webhook subscription, recreates the app with a new session secret plus previous-secret staging and a new webhook signing key, verifies readiness and API-key continuity, verifies new webhook subscriptions derive a different one-time signing secret, removes `SESSION_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, verifies readiness again, and scans Compose logs for old/new secrets plus one-time webhook secrets.
- Docker builds now use `.dockerignore` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` so local dependency folders are not copied into the image and installs run with `--frozen-lockfile`.
- Postgres repository composition now lives in the database package as a reusable factory with full contract method assertions; the app server delegates to that factory instead of carrying persistence composition logic locally.
- A repository conformance suite now runs against the in-memory repository by default and against isolated migrated pgvector Postgres databases when `ROMEO_POSTGRES_CONFORMANCE_DATABASE_URL` is set.
- `pnpm review:repository-conformance -- --strict --output /tmp/romeo-repository-conformance-coverage.json` now passes with direct shared-conformance references for all 229 repository contract methods and zero waivers.
- The repository contract now includes an explicit `transaction` boundary. In-memory transactions restore a rollback snapshot on failure, and the Postgres repository composes a transaction-scoped repository from the Drizzle transaction handle.
- The greenfield baseline lock is recorded in [Romeo Greenfield Baseline Lock](./baseline-lock.md); future schema changes require forward-only migrations with upgrade tests and rollback or mitigation notes.
- Agent publish now creates the immutable version, updates the agent's published pointer, and writes the sanitized publish audit in one repository transaction; failure-injection verifies an audit failure rolls back the version and pointer update.
- Run completion now updates terminal run state, records terminal usage, and creates the assistant message in one repository transaction; failure-injection verifies a terminal usage failure rolls back the completed state and assistant message. Webhook emission remains asynchronous after successful persistence so delivery failures do not block completion.
- Connector sync finalization now updates the sync row, advances connector cursor/schedule metadata, and writes the sanitized sync audit in one repository transaction; failure-injection verifies an audit failure leaves the sync running and the connector cursor unchanged. External fetch, object storage, and knowledge ingestion stay outside the DB transaction boundary.
- Workflow resume finalization now updates workflow run state and writes the sanitized resume/retry/recover audit in one repository transaction; failure-injection verifies a resume audit failure leaves a waiting run unchanged. Retry branches that create new linked model runs remain a deeper design item if strict linked-run creation atomicity is required.
- Governed data deletion and native chat deletion now delete scoped chat data and write sanitized deletion audits in repository transactions; failure-injection verifies an audit failure restores the chat and messages instead of leaving an unaudited destructive delete.
- Tool operation dispatch request enqueue idempotency, claim, lease renewal, timeout expiry, max-attempt dead-lettering, and cancellation now run through the repository/API/SDK/CLI contract with sanitized worker metadata only. Completion/failure readback requires an active worker lease, idempotency stores only scoped key hashes and request-shape metadata, cancellation stores `worker_cancelled` plus stable reason metadata, expiry stores `worker_dispatch_request_expired` plus stable reason metadata, stale requests reclaimed after three worker leases are failed with `worker_attempts_exhausted`, and terminal replay is rejected; failure-injection verifies an audit failure leaves claimed jobs retryable.
- `romeo workers tool-dispatch` now claims queued dispatch requests with either operator-supplied payload files or managed encrypted payloads. Without a payload file it requests only `managed_encrypted_object_store` jobs, reads raw execution payloads through the active-lease `POST /api/v1/tool-operation-dispatch-requests/{jobId}/payload` API, and leaves normal claim/job/audit/run surfaces metadata-only. With a payload file it can also execute legacy `external_worker_secret_store_required` jobs. The worker receives sanitized claim-time OAuth token policy and JSON response-schema subsets when imported operations declare them, resolves auth values from explicit `env`, `vault`, `aws-sm`, `gcp-sm`, `azure-kv`, or aggregate `cloud` resolver drivers, performs HTTPS requests with redirect failure, timeout and byte limits, denies local/private literal hosts, DNS-resolved private/link-local/reserved addresses, and Kubernetes service DNS names by default, validates bounded JSON response bytes locally, completes with metadata-only response readback, fails missing payloads with `worker_payload_unavailable`, fails missing worker secrets with `worker_secret_unavailable`, and keeps raw payload, auth tokens, OAuth client credentials, access tokens, and response bodies out of worker output. Backend/API managed encrypted object-store payload handoff is available behind `TOOL_DISPATCH_PAYLOAD_STORE_DRIVER=object-store`; enqueue, claim, run-event, and worker-readback metadata report `managed_encrypted_object_store` without exposing raw values or secret refs, and terminal transitions best-effort delete managed payload objects. Focused tests cover unsafe literal-host denial before fetch, DNS-resolved private-host denial before fetch, DNS lookup failure mapping, timeout mapping, response truncation, response schema pass/fail/invalid-JSON/truncated handling, redirect failure mapping, bearer/API-key/OAuth auth redaction, missing-secret failure, Vault/AWS/GCP/Azure/cloud resolver behavior, the payload-file/secret-resolver contract, managed payload queue/claim/read/lease-denial/cleanup, worker managed-payload execution, and model-originated managed encrypted payload redaction. Compose now has an opt-in `tool-dispatch` profile with resolver environment wiring, and Helm can render a `workers.toolDispatch` CronJob with optional Secret-mounted payload files plus schema-validated resolver driver, optional resolver credentials from the runtime Secret, and a separate component-scoped `workers.toolDispatch.networkPolicy` egress policy. Remaining production work is live CNI/NetworkPolicy egress enforcement and DNS-rebinding evidence plus live worker log/NetworkPolicy evidence capture.
- Pending tool approval queues are now API-backed through `GET /api/v1/tool-approvals` and `client.tool.approvals(...)`, returning unified metadata-only summaries for built-in tool-call approvals and imported operation dispatch approvals without new storage. The list is scoped to the caller, supports agent/run filters where those fields exist, includes source, tool name, risk, approval policy, input key names, connector/operation context, requested time, expiry, and available approve/cancel/reject actions, omits raw tool inputs and payload values, expires stale approval requests, and removes approvals after a later matching successful execution, operation consumption, or metadata-only decision. Caller-owned pending approvals can now be metadata-approved, cancelled, or rejected through `POST /api/v1/tool-approvals/{approvalRequestId}/approve`, `/cancel`, `/reject` and `client.tool.approveApproval(...)`, `client.tool.cancelApproval(...)`, `client.tool.rejectApproval(...)`; approved execution still uses the existing run-scoped tool execution contract with user-approved input. Built-in cancel/reject decisions record stable metadata in the existing background-job ledger, write sanitized audit metadata, cancel a waiting run with sanitized `tool.failed` plus `run.cancelled` events, and block later replay approval. Imported operation cancel/reject updates the existing approval job with sanitized decision metadata, writes a sanitized audit event, removes the request from the queue, and blocks later replay approval before network execution without adding a migration.

The migration inventory remains only `0000_greenfield_baseline.sql` plus Drizzle metadata.
- `pnpm smoke:compose:auth` now provides live local Compose evidence for the local-auth fallback contract, including one-time MFA recovery codes. It migrates and explicitly seeds a temporary stack, sets a local admin password, recreates the app with `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`, verifies readiness and unconfigured OIDC fail-closed behavior, proves local password login, HttpOnly session cookies, `/me.deployment.tenancyMode`, TOTP enrollment/confirmation, MFA-required login, invalid-code rejection, successful MFA verification, recovery-code generation, recovery-code challenge login, reused-code rejection, local-auth status readback with remaining-code counts only, audit redaction for password/TOTP/recovery-code values, and Compose log redaction for generated auth secrets. - The local auth/MFA fallback assertions now live in `scripts/lib/auth-smoke-support.mjs` and are reused by both Compose auth smoke and Kubernetes live smoke. `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:live` injects local MFA and managed-secret encryption keys, sets the local admin password before secure upgrade, then verifies local auth/MFA fallback, recovery-code behavior, and unconfigured OIDC fail-closed behavior after `DEV_SEEDED_LOGIN=false`; local validation passed script syntax checks, docs command-check, offline Kubernetes render smoke, and live Compose auth smoke through the shared helper. Live Kubernetes proof still needs a reachable cluster.
- Account linking remains disabled by policy, and OIDC JIT provisioning now fails closed for identity collisions without adding storage. If an incoming OIDC email matches a different same-org user, or if the deterministic issuer/subject user ID already belongs to another org, bearer and PKCE login reject instead of creating a second local account or moving an existing user. Focused API tests cover email collision and cross-org issuer/subject collision.
- Enterprise access review now has a broader redacted report through `GET /api/v1/access-review/report`, `GET /api/v1/access-review/report.csv`, `client.governance.accessReviewReport(...)`, and `client.governance.accessReviewReportCsv(...)` without adding storage. The report covers users, groups, group memberships, service accounts, active API-key/session counts, resource grants, data connector ownership, delegated OAuth connection posture, tool connector risk, worker job counts, support approval/session posture, and explicit identity policy posture while excluding API key hashes, session hashes, support reason text, connector configs, secret refs, OAuth token envelopes, background job payloads, and tool payloads. Support impersonation sessions can now be explicitly revoked by true admins through `POST /api/v1/admin/impersonation/sessions/{sessionId}/revoke` and `client.sessions.revokeSupportSession(...)`; revocation invalidates the backing hashed session token and writes `support.impersonation.revoke` audit metadata without reason text. Focused tests cover report content, support revocation, token invalidation, and redaction.
- Eval rubrics now support metadata-only `expectedToolOutcomes` alongside `expectedToolCalls`, and release-candidate evidence now aggregates expected-tool-call and expected-tool-outcome pass/fail counts plus failed tool-expectation case counts. Outcome checks read fenced `romeo-tool-outcome`, `tool-outcome`, `tool-result`, or `json` blocks and match tool name plus optional success/failure status, expected output-key subset, and stable error code; raw tool names, arguments, output keys, error codes, and result bodies are excluded from release-candidate evidence. Focused eval tests cover passing and failing outcome assertions plus evidence redaction without adding tables or migrations.
- Live model tool-call orchestration now has a bounded inline API/service path through `POST /api/v1/runs/{runId}/tools/{toolId}/execute` and `client.tool.executeForRun(...)`. The route derives the agent from the run, reuses existing tool binding/approval/quota/audit/tool-call/run-event behavior, and stores provider `modelToolCallId` replay guards only as scoped hashes plus metadata-only input keys in `background_jobs`; raw provider call IDs and raw arguments stay out of jobs and run events. Provider tool-call normalization now covers OpenAI-compatible chat `tool_calls`, streamed chat chunks, same-turn tool-call batches, Responses-style `function_call` items, and Ollama `message.tool_calls`. The OpenAI-compatible chat adapter uses resolved managed provider credentials for real `/chat/completions` SSE streaming, records provider usage chunks, normalizes streamed tool calls, serializes assistant/tool continuation messages, fails closed when a configured credential is unavailable, and returns only credential posture (`credentialConfigured`, `credentialRefScheme`) from provider APIs. The OpenAI Responses-compatible adapter is a first-class provider kind with default capabilities, provider creation/sync API coverage, `/responses` SSE streaming with `store:false`, typed function-call/function-call-output continuation items, streamed text/usage normalization, and fragmented function-call argument normalization. The Ollama adapter is now tool-capable through `/api/chat`: it sends streaming chat requests with optional bearer auth, injects tool definitions, normalizes JSON-line text/usage/tool-call chunks, serializes assistant/tool continuation history, and fails closed when a configured credential is unavailable while hermetic tests keep deterministic dev echo. Authorization-filtered registered tool schemas now flow into tool-capable Chat Completions, Responses-compatible, and Ollama provider requests for built-in tools and agent-bound worker-ready imported OpenAPI operations. Model-requested imported operations validate the `{ parameters, body }` envelope, use the operation row ID as the provider tool name, and enqueue existing `tool.operation.dispatch_request` jobs with connector/operation IDs, method/path, host, parameter/body key names, approval posture, worker queue, payload-storage posture, run linkage metadata, subject posture, and idempotency hash only; with managed payload storage enabled, raw model-provided operation payloads are written only to encrypted object-store envelopes before queueing and read by workers only through the active-lease payload API. Single or batched non-approval built-in model-requested tools execute through `ToolService` before provider generation resumes with bounded tool result content. Approval-gated model tool calls move runs into `waiting_tool_approval`, expose the server-issued `approvalRequestId` in sanitized run events, and resume provider generation after approved run-scoped inline tool execution. Model-requested imported operations now move runs into a metadata-only `run.waiting_tool_dispatch` wait, resume provider generation after worker completion/failure readback using the dispatch job ID as the synthetic continuation call ID, and cancel linked queued/running dispatch jobs when the run is cancelled. Approved tool-call resumes and worker-dispatch readbacks emit metadata-only `run.continuing` events with stable reason/job/approval/outcome metadata before generation continues. Completed, failed, or cancelled runs reject further run-scoped tool execution, and suspended approval or dispatch waits append terminal `run.cancelled` events without exposing raw provider call IDs, raw arguments, operation payload values, managed payload object keys, or worker response bodies. Remaining work is UI progress polish, target eval evidence, future provider-specific continuation beyond OpenAI-compatible, Responses-compatible, and Ollama if additional tool-capable adapters are introduced, and live worker/deployment evidence.

Selected OpenWebUI-shaped bridge shims exist only when `OPENWEBUI_COMPATIBILITY_ENABLED=true`; OpenWebUI remains a reference project rather than Romeo's product model. Native Romeo APIs are authoritative for new UI/product work, and further OpenWebUI exact-route parity requires explicit product approval.

- Delegated connector OAuth now has a GitHub provider registry, PKCE authorization-start API, public callback completion, encrypted token persistence, sanitized connection list/revoke APIs, metadata-only admin provider/connection posture, provider-side GitHub grant revocation, server-side callback replay rejection, and audit events through `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/providers`, `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/start`, `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/callback`, `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/connections`, `/api/v1/delegated-oauth/connections/{connectionId}/revoke`, and `/api/v1/admin/delegated-oauth/posture`. - Provider stream failures and idle timeouts now emit metadata-only `run.failed` events with stable error codes instead of persisting provider exception messages or delayed provider text. Provider runtime resilience now retries only pre-output stream failures/timeouts, avoids retrying after assistant content emits, opens a fail-fast circuit with `provider_circuit_open`, routes to a configured fallback model only before output, avoids calling kill-switched providers when a valid fallback exists, and is covered by `pnpm smoke:providers:resilience`.
- `/api/v1/providers/operational-summary`, TypeScript SDK `admin.providerOperationalSummary()`, CLI `romeo providers summary`, and the app Providers panel now expose metadata-only provider operational state for circuit snapshots, fallback availability, kill switches, model counts, provider status, and alert codes. The summary intentionally omits provider base URLs, raw prompts, raw provider errors, and provider payloads; production monitoring and live provider outage alert firing remain target-tier evidence.
- `pnpm monitoring:export` now converts provider and job operational summaries into Prometheus text metrics in one-shot or HTTP exporter mode. `deploy/monitoring/prometheus-rules.yaml` defines the matching provider/job alert rules, `deploy/monitoring/operational-exporter.deployment.example.yaml` provides a restricted Kubernetes Deployment/Service starting point, and `pnpm validate:operational-monitoring` proves metric/rule wiring plus raw sentinel redaction. Live alert firing in the selected Prometheus/Grafana/Alertmanager stack remains target-tier evidence.
- `deploy/monitoring/prometheus-rules.yaml` now includes `RomeoPostgresBackupJobFailed` using kube-state-metrics `kube_job_status_failed` for the Helm Postgres backup job path. `pnpm smoke:alerts:live` now collects `romeo.live-alert-firing.v1` evidence from a live Prometheus API, requires provider, queue-lag, dead-letter, and backup alert evidence by default, and optionally verifies Alertmanager active-alert readback. `pnpm smoke:alerts:contract` runs that live harness against loopback Prometheus and Alertmanager fixtures, proves missing required alert readback fails closed, and checks bearer-token redaction. The GA checklist rejects dry-run, contract-smoke, or non-firing alert evidence.

It keeps evidence bodies, exception rationale, raw evidence paths, logs, prompts, provider payloads, connector payloads, and secret-like environment values out of the bundle; `--require-readback-validation` makes readback evidence mandatory, and `--require-checklist-passed` implies that requirement while failing promotion if the GA checklist remains blocked.

## Product-Complete Blockers

These are GA blockers unless explicitly deferred for a narrow demo or prototype.

Execution tickets for these blockers now live under [Remaining Work Execution Backlog](./execution/README.md). Use this status file as the implementation ledger and the execution backlog files as the work breakdown.

1. Durable runtime live validation:
   - Initial live API readiness smoke now passes against a migrated isolated Postgres database.
   - The Compose app container/server path now passes live validation against a migrated Postgres database using the same runtime selection code that Compose uses.
   - Kubernetes app startup still needs live validation against a migrated Postgres database using the same contract.
   - Keep `REPOSITORY_DRIVER=memory` development-only.

2. Baseline migration review, restore testing, and lock:
   - The Phase 19 baseline is locked in [Romeo Greenfield Baseline Lock](./baseline-lock.md); strict static baseline review passes, and live schema validation passes before and after restore.
   - Generated SQL now has pgvector ordering, required cascade FKs, lifecycle columns, required tables/indexes, no destructive statements, and no over-limit FK names according to `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict`.
   - Compose Postgres and object-store backup/restore DR smoke now exists.
   - Avoid corrective migration chains after the baseline lock; future schema changes require forward-only migrations with upgrade tests.

3. Live Postgres conformance:
   - An initial reusable Postgres test harness now creates isolated databases, applies the greenfield baseline, seeds minimal fixtures, composes the durable repository, and cleans up.
   - Current shared conformance covers full contract method presence plus direct behavioral references for all 229 repository methods across the transaction boundary, tenancy/identity including organization create/update/read, auth credentials including local password and MFA factors, system settings for provider policy and tenant lifecycle, provider/model, agents/evals, chat/message/collaboration, notifications, knowledge/vector search and metadata updates, connectors, delegated OAuth connections and refresh locks, runs/tools, workflows, governance, billing/quota, webhooks, operational metadata, voice profiles, file object records, access grants, folders, favorites, bridge channel records/members, workspace creation, and governed deletion.
   - `pnpm review:repository-conformance -- --strict` now passes with zero uncovered methods and no waivers.
   - Failure-injection now covers agent publish, run completion terminal usage, connector sync finalization, workflow resume finalization, governed data deletion, tool-dispatch enqueue/approval consumption, tool-dispatch approval decisions, and tool-dispatch readback. Cross-org/cross-workspace negative authorization coverage now includes the remaining connector sync, workflow resume, and governed data deletion service paths.

4. Compose product install:
   - Compose now has app/Postgres/Valkey/RustFS services, a migration service, explicit development seed command, object-store bucket bootstrap, external Postgres override, worker/voice/knowledge/backup profiles, persistent Valkey storage, basic app/Postgres/Valkey health checks, an automated clean-project smoke for secure readiness plus app, Valkey, RustFS, and Postgres restart persistence, automated one-shot worker smoke plus selected loop-worker restart evidence, workflow-resume SIGKILL recovery evidence for controlled pending work, automated external-Postgres acceptance smoke, and automated Postgres plus object-store backup/restore/DR smoke.
   - API/repository raw-content redaction sentinel coverage, Compose app raw-content log sentinel scanning, Compose worker raw-content output/log sentinel scanning, staged OIDC session-secret rotation support, and live Compose secret-rotation smoke now exist. `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:log-redaction` now provides a focused Kubernetes pod/job log-redaction harness for operator-supplied raw prompt, provider-payload, worker-payload, and secret sentinels without storing those sentinel values. Live target execution remains required before using it as deployment evidence.

## Phase 19 Lock Ledger And Phase 20 Backlog

### P19-A: Compose Postgres Repository Runtime

Status: implemented at the app runtime boundary with live API readiness smoke against migrated Postgres and a passing production-like Compose app container smoke; pending Kubernetes startup-path validation.

Tasks:

- Create a `PgRomeoRepository` or factory that composes the focused fragments without introducing a catch-all persistence file.
- Add repository runtime metadata with `driver: "postgres"`, `durable: true`, and storage scope details.
- Add application startup selection that uses Postgres when `DATABASE_URL` is present in production and keeps in-memory only behind an explicit development/test path.
- Add a production readiness check that verifies durable repository metadata, schema validation state, and pgvector availability.
- Start the app against migrated Postgres with seeded login disabled and production-like secrets.

Definition of done:

- A production app boot cannot silently use in-memory state.
- The service layer receives the same `RomeoRepository` contract for memory and Postgres.
- No existing fragment grows into an unrelated multi-domain file.

Testing and validation:

- Unit test repository factory selection.
- API readiness test for Postgres runtime metadata.
- Negative readiness tests for missing `DATABASE_URL`, memory repository in production, and seeded login in production.
- Live API smoke against the composed Postgres runtime.
- App container/server smoke with `REPOSITORY_DRIVER=postgres`, migrated Postgres, seeded login disabled, and production-like secrets.

### P19-B: Baseline Migration Lock Candidate

Status: locked. The baseline candidate was generated, strict static-reviewed, live-applied through the shared migrator, backed up, restored into a second database, schema-validated after restore, and covered by strict repository method conformance plus transaction failure-injection evidence for publish, run completion terminal usage, connector sync finalization, workflow resume finalization, governed data deletion, tool-dispatch enqueue/approval consumption, tool-dispatch approval decisions, and tool-dispatch readback. The lock decision is recorded in [Romeo Greenfield Baseline Lock](./baseline-lock.md).

Tasks:

- Generate one Drizzle migration from the accepted schema.
- Review generated SQL for pgvector extension, foreign keys, natural unique constraints, indexes, lifecycle columns, identifier length, and deletion behavior through `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict`.
- Add a migration command and migration job entrypoint shared by Compose and Kubernetes.
- Add migration evidence output without logging database URLs or secrets.
- Keep shortened explicit foreign-key names for the few relationships that would otherwise exceed PostgreSQL's 63-byte identifier limit.

Definition of done:

- An empty database migrates to the accepted schema with one baseline file.
- `pnpm validate:postgres` succeeds against the migrated database.
- The migration file does not contain undo/redo churn from earlier schema mistakes.

Testing and validation:

- Empty database migration test.
- Schema validator live test.
- Static SQL review evidence from `pnpm review:baseline -- --strict --output ...`.
- Actual `pnpm migrate:postgres` run against an empty pgvector database.
- Backup, restore into a second database, and restored schema validation.

Transaction failure-injection now covers the repository transaction boundary, agent publish, run completion terminal usage, connector sync finalization, workflow resume finalization, governed data deletion, tool-dispatch enqueue/approval consumption, tool-dispatch approval decisions, and tool-dispatch readback. Cross-scope negative authorization evidence now covers the remaining connector sync, workflow resume, and governed data deletion service paths.

- Keep conformance suites for ordering, idempotency, uniqueness, authorization-facing lookups, lifecycle transitions, deletion, and redaction-sensitive metadata.

- Helm lint and values schema checks.
- `pnpm smoke:kubernetes:render` for default, external Postgres, CloudNativePG, HPA, NetworkPolicy, Ingress, worker, backup, restricted-pod, and optional KEDA example evidence.
- Documentation review for CloudNativePG and external hosted Postgres parity.
